> **MANDATORY MAINTENANCE INSTRUCTION — CODEX AND GITHUB COPILOT**
> Read this handover before changing the MWA project. After every code, configuration, database-rule, deployment, integration or documentation change, update this same file before handing work back to the user. Record what changed, why, affected files, checks and results, deployment/Git status, outstanding work and the next action. Update it even when work is incomplete or blocked, so another assistant can resume without this chat. Never mark planned work as implemented or an attempted deployment as successful. Keep credentials, donor/customer records and private email contents out of this file. Commit the handover with the associated changes when committing is authorised. Both assistants must preserve these instructions and maintain a single authoritative handover rather than separate competing copies.

## MWA.ai conversational form guide — 11 November 2026 — LOCAL, NOT ACTIVATED

User requested a floating mobile-friendly MWA.ai assistant, selected conversational Gemini through the MWA Google Cloud project, and clarified the key interaction: describe a task, open the relevant form, then fill it in. Implemented a Gemini callable and themed accessible chat dialog locally. Intent catalogue distinguishes Niaz money donation (`/?donationFund=niaz#donate`, preselects Niaz), Niaz sponsorship/majlis (`/niaz`, programme selection/verification preserved), and event booking (`/calendar/?action=new-booking`, existing BookingModal opens with verification and approval rules unchanged). Assistant never submits forms, takes payments or changes operational records. No event/date is invented or preselected from model output. Ambiguous sponsorship/donation/venue requests prompt clarification.

Code: Website/functions/mwa-ai.js, mwa-ai-catalog.json, mwa-ai.test.js; new `mwaAiChat` export in functions/index.js; Website/website/MwaAssistant.jsx, mwa-assistant.css, mwa-assistant-mount.jsx; firebase-bridge.jsx mount; donation-fields.js preselection; Centralised Calendar/src/pages/CalendarPage.jsx direct booking open. Published CMS text uses a small explicit allowlist; only current server-resolved module grants enter the navigation catalogue. No donor/enquiry/form responses enter prompts. Current permissions rechecked after generation. Chat uses local memory, clears on identity/permission changes, and is not stored by our app. User messages go to Gemini when activated; provider retention/logging must be reviewed separately. Text is plain React text; server returns only catalogue-owned URLs, never model-generated action URLs. Underlying page permissions remain authoritative.

Protections: Firebase callable App Check enforced; bounded messages/history/output; server-side Google credentials; UTC-day transactional limits of 30 per hashed network address, 4-second spacing, 500 project-wide. Usage counts (no chat text/raw IP) go to private `_mwaAiUsage`; unmatched Firestore paths deny client access. `expiresAt` is populated, but TTL must be configured before activation. These are request/token bounds, not a currency-budget guarantee. Max two instances / ten concurrent requests each / 40s function / 25s provider timeout. No tool execution or arbitrary URL fetching.

Activation is deliberately OFF until configured: build-time `VITE_MWA_AI_ENABLED=true` and runtime `MWA_AI_ENABLED=true`, `MWA_AI_MODEL=<supported Gemini model ID>`, `MWA_AI_LOCATION=global` required. No API key needed: runtime service-account OAuth. Read-only gcloud check found `aiplatform.googleapis.com` not enabled in project `project-7a4fb531-414f-4ade-88e`; active local gcloud identity is developer Sajjad, not MWA admin. No cloud API/IAM/configuration/production changes made in this increment. Gemini 2.5 Flash-Lite documentation lists retirement 20 October 2026, so do not use old tutorials' model ID. A supported model and actual access must be confirmed during activation; no live model call or model-quality acceptance yet.

Checks so far: 11/11 backend tests and 11/11 mocked Chromium checks passed, including form routing, mobile fit, clear/Escape/focus, failure retry, and identity-change suppression. Mobile light/dark screenshots in C:/Users/sajja/AppData/Local/Temp/mwa-ai-mobile*.png. ### Approved view matrix — implemented locally

| View | Show | Search/filter | Actions |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Sponsorship Requests | Applicant, selected programme, request and per-event decision | Name/contact, event/date/status | Review, approve/finalise, decline |
| Monthly Programme Dashboard | Event title/date, sponsors/donors, MWA arrangement state | Month, title, sponsorship status | Assign/edit/join sponsors; plan MWA arrangements |
| Recurring Commitments | Sponsor, stable programme series, next-year review state | Sponsor, programme, year, active/paused | Confirm next-year occurrences, pause, transfer |
| Sponsorship History | Past event assignments, decisions and changes | Sponsor, event, date range/status | Read details and export |

User approved this four-view structure, with Sponsor Directory remaining a separate utility tab. Recurring Commitments concerns future occurrences of ongoing commitments; Sponsorship History concerns individual occurrence assignments and is read-only, with Sydney-date range/search/status filters and filtered export. Requests also includes older staged assignment proposals so they remain reviewable. Lists are paginated to an explicit 10,000-row bound rather than silently filtering only the first page. Legacy Calendar sponsor export has been removed from the interface as requested (the unused read-only backend route remains).

### Consistent direct-admin approval policy — 11 November user instruction

Authorised Admin/Super Admin direct sponsor assignments and reviewed sponsor imports create approved assignments immediately, with no EOI/request or approval-email workflow. Existing sponsor eligibility, duplicate/sole/joint rules, permission checks, optimistic revisions and audit records remain enforced. Public EOIs still require coordinator review. Automatically generated future-year proposals and old staged proposals are not silently bulk-approved. New backend regression explicitly covers direct assignments/imports without EOI or approval mail.

Single admin booking creation now records confirmed status server-side; signed-in non-admin callers cannot gain approval by sending a status field. Admin venue clashes reject creation. Admin recurring series are also confirmed and preflight clashes reject the series. Confirmation mail replaces the pending-submission mail for new direct admin single bookings, without approval action links. Existing Excel booking import already defaults to confirmed; deliberately imported historical statuses and edits of existing pending/public requests are preserved (not silently approved). Existing pending records are not backfilled. Recurring preflight remains the existing query/batch mechanism, not a new atomic venue reservation guarantee.

Affected policy paths: Centralised Calendar/functions/bookingSubmission.js, index.js, new bookingSubmission.policy.test.js, src/components/BookingModal.jsx; Website/functions/niaz-api.js and its tests. Wider redesign includes new niaz-event-plan.js, NiazEventEditor.jsx, support-menu.js and the Niaz model/lifecycle/notifications/API/UI plus shared form/navigation components listed above. Source remains uncommitted/unpushed; no deployment or live settings/data change. Notifications remain OFF. A later approved release must include the affected Calendar booking callable/delivery handlers and Website Niaz API/notification workers as well as Hosting, not Hosting alone.

Local evidence in C:/Users/sajja/AppData/Local/Temp: mwa-final-regression.log (97 passed), mwa-admin-policy-tests.log (39 passed), mwa-niaz-browser-final.log (16 passed), mwa-form-regression-final.log (41 passed); browser drivers mwa-niaz-new-browser.cjs and mwa-form-regression.cjs. No full end-to-end real-provider acceptance is claimed. Public mobile-only sponsors still need an email address for email delivery. Remaining: live donation linkage below, production acceptance/release when authorised, and broader interaction coverage for populated Insights/donation cancellation flows not exercised by the mocked fixtures.

### New donation linkage requirement (recorded; NOT implemented as live integration)

Successful general Niaz Fund donations must create/match an Active directory donor. Successful event-specific Niaz donations must also create an approved event association labelled Niaz Donor (contributor); coordinator determines sole/joint classification, with no invented automatic amount threshold. Public naming still requires consent. Use verified server settlement, stable donor matching, idempotency and refund/reversal handling; setup-only/awaiting payments do not qualify. Never infer email/mobile ownership solely from payment data. Current Stripe verification explicitly permits sandbox sessions only, and no authoritative live ledger adapter exists. No test donor has been converted into an actual/public sponsor. Implement and test the live adapter as a separate step once the real settlement source and consent capture are agreed; do not imply this new requirement is delivered by the existing finance-private TEST preview.

## Apple Pay sandbox investigation — 10 November 2026, Australia/Sydney — PAUSED by user, cause unresolved

**User-directed pause (15:04 Australia/Sydney).** User selected "Pause the investigation here" after the Stripe diagnostic-access blocker was explained. No fix or successful Apple Pay test is claimed. Resume only on user direction, preferably with Stripe Support's non-sensitive Checkout-session diagnostic findings. Documentation-only changes remain local/uncommitted/unpushed; no new deployment or provider/configuration/data change.

User reports Apple Pay sheet opens on the phone, displays that payment cannot be processed, then returns to Stripe's payment-method page. User confirms a normal bank-issued card in Apple Wallet, not an Apple sandbox/test card. This supersedes any assumption that the button is absent; it does not establish the reason for failure.

**Read-only findings.** Canonical checkout initially clean on released docs commit `39f8c9064da96a8dfd0be695ccaf2c5011ed0d19`. Inspected `Website\website\donations.js`, `Website\functions\donations.js`, `donations-runtime.js` and prior sandbox checkpoints. App redirects to Stripe-hosted `https://checkout.stripe.com/`; Apple Pay is not in the excluded method list (Link alone is explicitly hidden). Runtime rejects non-test secret prefixes and unexpected live-mode sessions. No application Apple Pay-sheet handler exists to fix locally on present evidence. Stripe's current official web Apple Pay documentation (`https://docs.stripe.com/apple-pay?platform=web`) says hosted Checkout needs no additional domain registration, supports recurring payments, and testing uses a normal Wallet card with test API keys (no real charge). Therefore neither unregistered MWA domain nor the confirmed normal Wallet card is an established cause. Historical 2 November evidence only observed wallet visibility, not successful wallet payment.

**Control tests (user-confirmed, not agent-tested).** Google Pay and manual Stripe test-card checkout both work. This narrows the reported problem to Apple Pay rather than a general sandbox Checkout failure; it does not establish Apple merchant validation or QR/device compatibility. No payment was initiated by the agent.

**Additional user evidence.** Failure is specifically the desktop-to-phone **QR flow**: immediately after scanning, the phone starts processing without reaching a side-button approval; after roughly 1–2 minutes it says **"Payment not Completed"**, desktop "continue on phone" closes, and the hosted method chooser returns with no visible desktop error. This suggests (not proves) a pre-authorisation handoff/session failure, rather than a confirmed card decline. Apple's current guidance (`https://support.apple.com/en-us/102626`, "How to use Apple Pay by scanning a code") requires scan, review, then side-button/Face ID/Touch ID/passcode confirmation; scanning alone is not payment authorisation. **Direct-phone isolation result (user-confirmed, not agent-tested):** opening Checkout directly in iPhone Safari also fails in the same way, but faster. Therefore the failure is not confined to the desktop QR handoff. The exact failed Apple Pay stage remains unconfirmed; Wallet/device or Stripe Apple Pay session/payment-method evidence is needed before assigning a cause. Google Pay and manual test-card success do not establish that the Apple Pay-specific path is configured or operating correctly. Do not weaken app payment checks or configure merchant certificates for Stripe-hosted Checkout based on this symptom.

**Dashboard observation (user-confirmed, not agent-tested).** User found no matching Apple Pay attempt in the sandbox Transactions list around the failure. This does not by itself establish that Stripe received no request: this integration uses Checkout `mode: 'setup'` for recurring-only donations, which need not create an immediate payment entry. User subsequently confirmed the Niaz attempt was **one-off**: Apple Pay failed, then Google Pay succeeded in that checkout. Another Apple Pay attempt concerned Khums (cadence/result not yet established). The Niaz case therefore uses payment-mode Checkout, not recurring-only setup mode. A separate failed row is still not required: attempts within one Checkout can share a PaymentIntent and its final successful Transactions row. Next inspect the successful Google Pay payment's timeline/failure details around the earlier Apple Pay attempt; absent such evidence, the Apple Pay failure stage remains unresolved. Recurring-only Checkout/SetupIntent evidence is not the next diagnostic for this confirmed one-off case. Do not infer a confirmed bank decline or a proven device-only fault.

**Wallet control (user-confirmed, not agent-tested).** The same iPhone/Wallet card works for Apple Pay on other websites/apps. This reduces the likelihood of a general Wallet/device failure, but does not prove compatibility with this particular Stripe sandbox session. User reiterates no Apple Pay-labelled transaction in any Transactions status. Do not keep requesting those same filters. Current evidence points to an Apple Pay-specific Checkout/session path, not a proven cause. Next actionable escalation is Stripe Support within the same sandbox, using the existing Checkout session (or successful Google Pay payment reference to locate it), failure timestamp and the before-authorisation symptom; request Apple Pay merchant-session/tokenisation diagnostics. Do not expose private session URLs or keys, create another payment, or register domains/certificates speculatively. Stripe hosted Checkout handles merchant validation; current documentation was rechecked.

**Diagnostic limit.** Attempted scoped read-only retrieval of the existing sandbox credential for redacted recent PaymentIntent/charge error codes and payment-method configuration. Google Secret Manager returned HTTP **403**; no credential payload, Stripe payment records or configuration could be retrieved. This is a permission/access blocker, not evidence that the deployed function cannot access its own secret or that Apple Pay is disabled. No IAM/account/provider/settings changes attempted. Do not infer a decline reason, enable wallets blindly, switch to live keys or create/retry a payment. Next evidence needed: failed attempt's sandbox Dashboard error/decline code and timestamp, with personal/card details hidden; if no attempt appears, investigate pre-submission Apple Wallet/device failure instead.

**State/evidence.** No app source/configuration change, commit, push or deployment; notifications unchanged/OFF. No payment/session/customer creation or emails. Only this same handover changed locally to record the partial investigation. Session-only diagnostic script: `C:\Users\sajja\.copilot\session-state\90de9ff7-c964-40a0-b2a8-b44acfe33078\files\apple-pay-sandbox-diagnostic.cjs`; it outputs only allowlisted codes/hashed references if authorised, but current attempt stopped before Stripe access. No tests/build needed for documentation-only work. The verified Niaz Hosting release below remains unchanged.

If "Item D" meant something else, those items are still open.

**Live checks after release (all read-only: no POST, no mail, no data or configuration write):**
- `/`, `/niaz/`, `/niaz/?event=…`, `/admin-login` and `/calendar/` return 200 HTML; `/calendar-app/` returns 200.
- Unauthenticated `/api/niaz/session`, `/settings`, `/mine`, `/eois`, `/notifications`, `/dashboard` and `/sandbox-ledger` return **401**. Public `/api/niaz/events?date=…` returns 200 `{"items":[]}`; `/api/niaz/public?eventId=<unknown>` returns 404. Existing `/api/youtube-feed` and `/api/website-content` return 200.
- All **16 distinct Niaz Firestore query shapes** (the four composite-index queries, the `orderBy('__name__')` pagination and the expiry sweeps) were run against live Firestore as key-only, limit-bounded `runQuery` reads. None reported a missing index. The local emulator does not enforce indexes, so this closes that gap. The Niaz collections are empty (0 documents).
- Logs: no WARNING or ERROR from any new or changed function after the deploy through 11:36Z. The first `niazNotificationWorker` Scheduler run at 11:33:00Z returned HTTP 200 in 1.2 s. `niazReminderPreparation` had not yet run its first hourly cycle when this was recorded; check its logs after that run.
- **Not verified:** authenticated Niaz UI against production, real login, SMS/OTP, Gmail delivery and payments.

**Notifications are OFF.** Both schedulers return `{disabled:true}` and no Niaz mail can be sent. Only the owner account (hard-coded `OWNER_EMAIL`, `Website/functions/adminAccess.js:11`; `niaz-notifications.js:12`) can enable delivery. "Disabled" means no notices, **not no writes**: `niazReminderPreparation` first prunes up to 100 expired `_niazImports` / `_niazLimits` rows per run, and the two Firestore triggers stay active regardless of the setting (`onNiazEventChanged` reads Niaz coverage on every `events` write; `niazSandboxDonationProjection` fires on every `_stripeSandboxDonations` write). Do not enable notifications until MWA accepts the permanent-retention and service-notice terms and a controlled provider acceptance test is approved.

Download the 500-row import template (`mwa-niaz-import-template.xlsx`). Sponsors use the public `/niaz/` EOI route with email/mobile verification. **Manual checks only the user can do, still unverified:** real SMS/OTP for booking and for Niaz EOI mobile under enforced App Check (the first OTP may wait for the App Check token exchange), the authenticated Niaz UI on production, and Gmail delivery (stays OFF).

**Still true, do not overclaim:** no authoritative membership source (member linking is off); Stripe is TEST-only (no live settlement, refund or funding, so scenarios 6/7/8/9 are partial); scenarios 1/4/10/12 are partial (they need verified membership); scenario 11 has injected-sender proof only; scenarios 2/3/5/13 have local evidence only. Minor open review items: `niaz-read`/`niaz-finance` readers can list sponsor contacts and the outbox; replaying an EOI returns the stored EOI including `ownerKey`/`requestHash`; the public `/events` and `/public` GETs are not rate limited; own history can show the title/date of an event that later became private; coordinator-recorded consent has provenance only in the audit log; a dead `'/eois'` entry remains in the generic collection map. Hashed image assets are still served `no-cache` (optional follow-up).

**Pending, each needing explicit approval:** (1) enabling Niaz notifications (owner-only action plus MWA policy acceptance); (2) a read-only production recheck, then the 12-booking conflict-flag repair (`recomputeAllConflicts` touches all venues); (3) the Hijri no-mail settings re-save test (a live admin action); (4) App Check option 2 (a security trade-off, NOT approved); (5) authoritative membership/live-ledger adapters; (6) optional immutable caching for hashed images. Push/deploy approval does not authorise IAM changes, booking repair, notification activation or data writes.

## Public Events & Bookings payload release — 9 November 2026, Australia/Sydney

User approved commit, push and website-only deploy of the reviewed payload change below. Source commit `9553568fe227256befcd4f09e079d5c9c05ab986` ("Serve embedded website images as cacheable assets") was pushed to `origin/main`. Coordinator review before release: no `img-src` CSP exists in Hosting headers or meta tags. CMS runtime only writes the logo `src` from `config.logo` and never reads DOM image sources. Tests passed **23/23, zero skipped**; a fresh Website plus embedded Calendar build passed; emitted `dist/index.html` was 308,735 bytes with no data-URL `<img src>`.

Pre-release rollback target, read from the live channel: release `1794205018500000` / version `f06cd4548ca6b1c0`. Deployed **only** `hosting:website` (`firebase deploy --only hosting:website --project project-7a4fb531-414f-4ade-88e --non-interactive`). New live release `1794205745547000` / version `5aa7d47bb211fe48` at 2026-11-09T06:29:05.547Z. No functions, rules, indexes, Calendar Hosting target, data or provider writes.

Live verification (read-only, signed out):
- `/calendar/`, `/` and `/admin-login` return 200, each 308,735 decoded bytes (162,241 transferred), with zero data-URL `<img>` tags. They reference `/assets/embedded-20a6a0890c8fc9cf.jpg`, `embedded-9250da517eca6727.jpg`, `embedded-b25c2b29b3a2917e.png` and `embedded-f237b2752a41d5cd.png`.
- All four live images match the built bytes (sizes 39,523 / 68,863 / 490,247 / 135,406; SHA256 equal).
- `/calendar-app/` returns 200.
- The images are served with existing catch-all `Cache-Control: no-cache` plus ETag, so they revalidate (304) rather than being immutable-cached like hashed JS/CSS. Adding `png|jpg|webp` to the immutable rule in `Website/firebase.json` would be an optional, separately approved follow-up.

Live timing, from the app Browser panel, clicking Events & Booking from `/admin-login` with cached JS (single samples, not benchmarks):
- HTML responseEnd **846 → 301 ms**.
- DCL **1043 → 348 ms**.
- Calendar frame request **1028 → 341 ms**.
- Frame FCP **~1714 → 1127 ms**.
- Load **3141 → 1721 ms**.

**Remaining public-calendar delay: App Check first token.** In the same sample `getPublicEvents` only started at ~5141 ms and ended at 5533 ms. The frame first waited for the reCAPTCHA Enterprise anchors (done ~1.7 s) and then for `exchangeRecaptchaEnterpriseToken` (started ~4872 ms). On reload with a valid cached App Check token there was no exchange, and `getPublicEvents` ran 1909–2134 ms. Headless Chromium samples showed the first-token wait much longer (events start 3.5–15.5 s), but headless reCAPTCHA timing is not representative of real users.

The frame performs **two** reCAPTCHA anchors and **two** token exchanges per visit. Cause: `Centralised Calendar/src/services/phoneBookingAuth.js:11-12` creates a second named Firebase app with its own `initializeAppCheck`, alongside `src/firebase/firebase.js:10-12`.

Candidate follow-ups, none implemented or approved:
1. Initialise the phone-booking app/App Check lazily, only when phone verification starts.
2. Review whether public read-only event listing must wait for App Check (security/abuse trade-off, e.g. a cached public events feed) — needs the user's decision.
3. Optionally apply immutable caching for hashed images.

Do not relax App Check without explicit approval.

Earlier remaining costs still apply: the ~201k-character inline CMS script and initial Website JS. Authenticated production login, Events & Bookings after sign-in, and live response-card interaction remain unverified.

## Admin performance and back navigation — 9 November 2026, Australia/Sydney

**Confirmed local causes and fixes:** the Website entry eagerly loaded all admin workspaces and the homepage community calendar. Admin workspaces, registration surfaces and that calendar now load on demand; embedded Calendar administration pages also use lazy loading with explicit workspace loading/error feedback. Calendar avoids an unnecessary public events request during auth loading and omits unused directory/Hijri listeners for Tools entry. Parent Website and same-origin Calendar frame previously performed two independent bootstrap `syncAdminAccess` calls (which write access/profile state and audit); they now share one identity-bound bootstrap result. Quiet response-viewer rechecks and live Firestore permission listeners remain independent. No backend, rules, App Check or reCAPTCHA protection was changed.

The original `AuthContext` allowed a delayed access-denial result from an earlier session to sign out a newer identity. A deterministic local Chromium fixture using the original HEAD source reproduced the new identity being signed out and redirected Home; the changed source ignored the stale result and stayed in Events & Bookings. Async sync outcomes now carry an immutable UID/email/verification/provider identity and generation fence, with bootstrap reset and syncing cleanup on sign-out/auth transitions. This confirms a local race and fixes it without arbitrary delays or weaker guards; it does **not** establish that every reported production bounce had this cause. Real sign-out, access loss, verified email, UID-bound allowlist, provider, expiry and module restrictions are preserved.

**Navigation:** one accessible, icon-only, top-left back control replaces redundant console/back panels in the Admin Console/login, CMS editor/gate, Donations, Form Builder, all three Insights workspaces, Enquiries, Events & Bookings administration, Calendar Management and Tools/Admin Team. Bounded session history records only validated in-app admin routes; the main console returns Home, direct module entries fall back to the console, and login return targets are normalized to avoid redirect loops. Calendar frame back messages require the exact frame and same origin. Nested Calendar administration, Tools/team, forms/preview/responses and enquiry views step back locally first. Form/response and team-access editing have discard confirmation; team saving disables Back. Enquiry draft confirmation and CMS unpublished-change confirmation are retained on the replacement controls. Public navigation, functional cross-module links and sign-in/sign-out actions remain. CMS uses shared-control portals over the existing legacy callbacks, without changing the generated inline HTML.

**Repeatable local measurements:** same-checkout `Website` `npm run build` before/after, not live latency: Website initial entry **1,049,655 → 844,477 bytes** (19.5% smaller), build-reported gzip **365.54 → 310.99 kB** (14.9% smaller), initial CSS **50.29 → 30.92 kB**. The approximately 1.287 MB Website HTML and full-document route navigations remain; no claim that all perceived lag is solved.

### Provisional deployment and rollback sequence — plan only

This is **not yet the exact full-release manifest**: the complete mixed-diff review and function-by-function impact mapping remain unfinished. Do not execute it or fill in missing targets by using a broad `functions` deployment. In particular, deploying a new scheduler or trigger can cause real production activity even when the intent is only to ship code.

1. Finish review and verification of tracked **and untracked** code, decide which functions, rules, indexes and flags belong in the release, and obtain explicit approval for the final commit, push and deployment together, plus any required IAM changes. Check availability and expiry of the current function source generations/configuration and both Hosting versions above; obtain separate approval to preserve verified rollback artifacts and configuration, and abort the release if viable restoration cannot be established. Recheck secret versions and actual runtime principal/bindings without reading values. `MWA_R2_ACCESS_KEY_ID` and `MWA_R2_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY` are required for `websiteCmsApi`; `YOUTUBE_DATA_API_KEY` is required for `youtubeFeedRefresh`. Provisioning bindings or grants is an IAM change, not implicitly approved.
2. Before dependent backend/client changes, deploy only the specifically reviewed required Firestore indexes from `Centralised Calendar` with `firebase deploy --only firestore:indexes --project project-7a4fb531-414f-4ade-88e --non-interactive` if the final manifest requires them, and wait until they are ready. The coordinating session's read-only comparison found all 29 live composite indexes READY, including an older `events(venueId,status,startAt)` absent from the earlier local file; that definition is now retained locally, and two duplicate local entries removed. All four booking TTL policies were already ACTIVE live. The local `responses.eventId` override now preserves the default collection array-contains index alongside the new collection-group ascending index. Recheck the live difference immediately before any separately approved index change; no index or TTL was deployed in this work. If rules are required, deploy `firestore:rules` only after verifying the compatible admin sync/UID-binding rollout sequence and preserving the previous rules; a rules deploy can change access immediately. Do not assume index readiness merely because deploy starts.
3. Minimum named Calendar backend targets already identified are `functions:importBookings,functions:previewRecurringBooking,functions:getAdminEvents`. The first two repair the observed old query ordering; `getAdminEvents` supports all-years Bookings. From a reviewed and frozen Calendar package, the candidate targeted command is `firebase deploy --only 'functions:importBookings,functions:previewRecurringBooking,functions:getAdminEvents' --project project-7a4fb531-414f-4ade-88e --non-interactive`. This **does not cover** the other changed Calendar booking, access, email, viewer, notification or shared-query consumers; determine and explicitly list their targets before an actual whole-release command. Backend and frontend must remain compatible throughout. Never re-import the two historical files.
4. Minimum identified Website backend targets, from the reviewed frozen Website package, are `functions:mwa-website:websiteCmsApi,functions:mwa-website:youtubeFeedRefresh`; the first serves the new form/Insights/poster endpoints and the second is a new scheduled provider reader. Release-time deployment of that scheduler and the associated IAM grant require explicit approval; do not activate Insights ingestion, backfill, cleanup, rules, scheduler or production flags merely by inference. The new `registrationInsightsIndex`, `registrationInsightsFormCatalog`, and `insightsMultiSnapshotCleanup` have production write/scheduler effects and must remain excluded until explicitly approved and included in a reviewed activation plan. Exact runtime options and remaining backend targets are still subject to the full release review.
5. After backend compatibility and required indexes are established, build `Website`, verify the YouTube feed has items, and propose the explicit `hosting:website` target from `Website`; it includes the embedded Calendar. The current live-to-local comparison above supports excluding the unchanged `hosting:calendar` redirect. Conduct only separately authorized post-deploy smoke checks, including public page and form rendering, read-only booking/calendar access, YouTube feed/player and R2 poster delivery; no real messages, payments, bookings, uploads or other writes without appropriate explicit authorization. Verify provider access and report actual outcome, not assumed success.
6. On failure, stop further stages. Use the preserved prior Hosting versions and verified prior function source/options to restore affected targets in a compatibility-safe order; confirm live revision and function health. If the required old artifact or configuration is unavailable, stop and escalate rather than redeploying guessed source. Rolling back import functions to the old versions also restores the known ordering defect and does **not** undo imported data. Do not roll back rules/indexes blindly or delete data; assess forward/backward compatibility and retain any indexes needed by restored services.
7. The historical 12 missing conflict flags are an observed earlier read-only estimate, not a current verified count. A separate approved read-only recheck and a reviewed, scoped repair plan with backup, audit and post-repair comparison are required before any write. The existing `recomputeAllConflicts` touches all venues and may change other rows; it is **not** an approved 12-row-only repair. Do not re-import files, fabricate summary audits or claim a repair occurred.

**Hijri release gate:** the earlier local version committed dates before conflict refresh and could strand later work. The user explicitly chose to delay release until the new Hijri update is failure-safe, not to exclude the trigger, and chose observable admin review rather than automatic retry for uncertain deliveries. The revised local implementation and failure-injection checks below address that scenario, but the latest generation/deadline revision requires the coordinator's independent follow-up and the full release manifest remains unfinished. No production behavior has been changed or verified.

**Local Hijri implementation, not deployed:** `Centralised Calendar/functions/hijriRecovery.js`, `index.js`, `mailer.js`, `gmailBookingMail.js`, `firestore.rules`, `firestore.indexes.json`, and `src/components/HijriRecoveryStatus.jsx` / `HijriSettings.jsx` implement durable runs, fenced conflict repair and admin-held mail. Each page's dates, deterministic audit and recovery tasks commit together; workers repair both old and current conflict windows, including clearing a cancelled booking's stale flags. A run is bound to the source settings snapshot's Firestore `updateTime` seconds and nanoseconds, not just its values. Scan and send-marker transactions compare the live generation: committed work still drains, but stale, never-sent notices become `not-sent` admin holds. Every settings document write starts a generation-bound run, even if overrides are unchanged; that can cause additional full scans and manual-review holds. An out-of-order deletion invocation transactionally creates `{overrides:[]}` only if the settings document remains absent, with a durable system audit record; it starts no run itself, and the resulting creation event starts the normal revision-bound run. Repository reader/writer inspection found the Calendar backend, Calendar settings service and Website community-calendar consumer use empty override defaults for missing and empty settings; the `changeHijriMonth` writer also starts from empty overrides. This is not a claim about external consumers.

The new scheduler resumes pending work every five minutes. Uncertain sends remain held and never automatically resent; known-unsent failures are labeled for manual review without blocking later pages. The bounded admin digest remains one per 100-event page. The shared worker deadline is rechecked inside each send-marker transaction attempt; inadequate budget leaves the task pending and releases its lease normally. A send marker's lease extends at least 60 seconds beyond its `notAfter` window; Gmail checks and abort timeouts bound the attempt, but cannot cancel or fence an already-dispatched request at the provider. A request dispatched before the deadline may be processed late, and absence from Sent does **not** prove manual resend is safe. Admins must review before any manual action. Review resolution is audited and does not send mail. Recovery task and review documents retain requester contact details in private collections; no retention period has been defined.

The latest demo Firestore and Gmail command ran 28 tests, all passing with no skips, including two reproductions that failed before the revision fix (A-to-B-to-A sent six rather than four messages; elapsed budget sent rather than left pending), then passed after it. Added checks cover nanosecond generations, out-of-order delivery, deletion/recreation and concurrent recreation, unchanged overrides, simulated transaction-callback replay with elapsed time, crash/refresh failures, lost acknowledgement, mailbox outage and uncertain aborted send through the actual mail wrapper. The callback replay is deterministic but is not an injected real Firestore transaction abort. An earlier combined recovery/mail/rules run passed 28 tests, including private collection write denial; it predates the generation follow-up. The synthetic Chromium review/resolve interaction and Website build including the embedded Calendar passed after the UI wording change; the build retains existing large-chunk warnings. Owner/read-only Admin Team checks passed 20 with one emulator-dependent case separately covered by rules. The 29 skipped no-emulator Website cases were covered in a full demo emulator run of 142 passing with no skips; Calendar's nine earlier no-emulator skips were separately exercised by demo emulator runs. These are local synthetic checks, not provider or production verification.

**Hijri rollout prerequisite, plan only:** after explicit release approval, deploy the reviewed indexes first and wait until `_hijriRecoveryRuns(state,nextAttempt)` is READY, then deploy compatible rules and the individually named functions `onHijriSettingsChanged`, `retryHijriRecovery`, `getHijriRecoveryStatus` and `resolveHijriNotificationReview` as part of the final reviewed manifest. Deploying `retryHijriRecovery` creates a Cloud Scheduler job that runs every five minutes. Existing settings changes do not spontaneously start a new run merely because the function is deployed: a subsequent settings write is needed. Permanent oversized-task, excessive-neighbor or excessive-venue failures remain visible as paused and retry periodically. Rollback artifacts and configuration must be preserved and restoration verified before release, with an abort gate if unavailable. No broad Functions deployment or unauthorized Insights activation.

**Parallel read-only release review:** scoped reviewers examined Calendar backend, both frontends, Website backend, and configuration/rules/Hosting. Calendar backend and frontend reviewers reported no significant findings in their scoped review; the Hijri files were separately reviewed and are not included in that assertion. The earlier recommendation to include both Hosting targets is superseded by the live-to-local comparison above. The newer count-only live inventory above found no unverified admins. Do not silently relax the requirement or claim a production outage. ## Booking import uncertain-result warning — 7 November 2026

The earlier two Excel imports committed bookings before a conflict-refresh error, making a generic `INTERNAL` misleading and repeat confirmation dangerous. Local `Centralised Calendar/functions/bookingImport.js` and `index.js` now wrap commit, conflict refresh and summary-audit stages: failures log the stage plus bounded sanitized error code and type, without message, stack or booking payload, and return a safe callable error warning that some or all bookings may already have been saved and must be checked before importing again. This deliberately does not claim a rollback, successful refresh or audit. `Centralised Calendar/src/pages/BookingsPage.jsx` also displays that conservative warning for any failed confirmation, including a network failure whose commit status cannot be known, removes the current confirmation preview and triggers a bookings reload. It does not prevent deliberate re-upload; the two previously imported files must not be re-imported. Full import idempotency and concurrent duplicate prevention are not established.

The 27 import/batch tests passed, including failures at all three stages, sanitized diagnostics and success ordering; the Calendar build and server syntax check passed after the UI reload refinement, with the existing large-chunk warning. The 651-event demo fixture now additionally returns exactly 50 Unknown-category events when type narrows the scope to 50, records 50 review entries, and rejects the result after another calendar event changes into that type. No real R2 call occurred. No claim of whole-project readiness or final diff review is made.

**Poster fencing research, not implemented:** Cloudflare's public [R2 consistency documentation](https://developers.cloudflare.com/r2/reference/consistency/) states `PutObject` conditions are evaluated atomically at commit and `If-None-Match: *` allows only one create for an absent key; a negative read alone does not rule out an earlier in-flight write. A possible future protocol is conditional create for every writer plus a durable object-key tombstone established after a Firestore intent claim and live-reference check; it must account for both already-committed and delayed writes, retries, crashes, every writer/version, conditional replacement and the fact that deleting the tombstone can permit a delayed create. Existing unconditional uploads are not fenced by this idea. The tombstone's retention/cost and any treatment of pre-existing unconditional writes require an explicit operational decision and proof before enabling cleanup. This is not an implemented or provider-tested recovery and does not authorize R2 writes.

## Bookings all-years view — 7 November 2026 (local only)

The user approved loading all years in the admin Bookings tab. `Centralised Calendar/functions/index.js` now keeps the existing `events-bookings` permission check on every `getAdminEvents` page and adds an explicit `allTime: true` mode using `functions/adminBookingQuery.js`: document-ID-ordered cursor pages of at most 200, independent of the Calendar month. The existing ranged admin/public queries and their 366-day maximum are unchanged. The separate admin Bookings feed in `src/App.jsx`, `src/services/eventsService.js` and `src/services/adminEventPages.mjs` follows up to 50 pages (10,000 bookings), checks for invalid dates, bad/repeated cursors and an explicit all-time scope acknowledgement, and displays an error rather than a partial result. The acknowledgement prevents an older server that ignores `allTime` from silently displaying its one-year default. Loading is cancelled between pages on tab, session or permission change; the calendar feed remains range-based and public Bookings keeps its previous public range. Reloads after changes preserve the mounted Bookings controls while hiding stale results. Client live-conflict calculation in `src/utils/conflictUtils.js` now prunes comparisons beyond the earlier event's buffered end, preserving the existing rule while avoiding comparisons across distant dates; very dense overlaps remain potentially quadratic.

The all-years limit is a client safety ceiling, not a server-side date filter: above it the user must contact an administrator; the current local date filters cannot narrow a failed all-years fetch. Invalid booking dates likewise block the complete list with a visible error. Paging is not a transactionally frozen snapshot: concurrent inserts, edits or deletions may require refreshing, and live capacity beyond the tested fixture has not been measured. A future release must deploy a compatible `getAdminEvents` function and client together; the old live function and live Hosting remain unchanged. No deployment is authorized or claimed.

## Excel booking import findings — 7 November 2026

The coordinating session's read-only investigation found that two Excel imports showed `INTERNAL` after Confirm at **05:47 UTC** and **06:47 UTC**. Each run nevertheless persisted 64 events and 64 per-row import audits: **128 events total**, with no duplicates within or across runs. Run 1 covered 15 June 2026–30 May 2027; run 2 covered 5 June 2027–18 May 2028. The deployed `importBookings` and `previewRecurringBooking` still use the old `loadConflictScope` query ordering: `startAt`, then document ID, without `endAt`. Firestore rejects it with `INVALID_ARGUMENT` because fields cannot be ordered after the document key; the callable masks that as `INTERNAL`. This happens after bookings commit, so the subsequent conflict refresh and `import_summary` audit did not run. Canonical source already has the ordering fix; the other 10 deployed consumers were found to be correct. The coordinator's read-only calculation found **12 imported bookings with missing conflict flags**; no repair was performed.

**Do not re-import these files.** Conflict-flag repair requires separate user approval. The error should be changed to a safe message explaining that bookings may already have been saved. A proposed surgical deployment, **plan only and not approved**, would run `firebase deploy --only functions:importBookings,functions:previewRecurringBooking` from frozen old deployed source with only the ordering line fixed. No deployment, repair or production write is authorized by this note. There is no 50-row booking cap: server pages contain up to 200 rows and the client follows cursors; the observed counts reflected the Calendar's 365-day window. The user has separately approved a local fix for the Bookings tab's all-years view.

## Resumable event inventory — 7 November 2026 (local)

The aggregate-only event inventory now has a gated 24-hour snapshot path sharing the existing temporary job, authorization, generation fences, calendar ledger and cleanup. It pages current Sydney-date calendar events and scans matching event-index records in 100-record batches and 30-event query groups. Private temporary membership contains event projections, form identifiers and counts, not answer/contact payloads. Accessible zero-indexed events remain visible and coverage remains incomplete; source counts are still checked for only the first 100 accessible events. Before completion and on each result read, a transactional current-range query plus ledger validation detects changed, added, deleted or moved calendar events. The UI rechecks the aggregate report and snapshot before displaying both together, checks current-event-date fingerprint parity, exposes cancellation and the limits, and retains the original explicitly lower-bound inventory when both gates are off. Aggregate-only access does not confer people or export permissions.

The snapshot refuses more than **600 calendar documents including inaccessible events**, **50,000 matching indexed records**, or an oversized form-id projection, rather than returning a partial inventory. The 600-calendar cap is lower than the legacy inventory's 5,000-event ceiling and is an explicit current tradeoff, not a claim of full three-year capacity. Global source/index generation fences remain subject to asynchronous trigger lag and out-of-band bypasses. The overall aggregate report and mapped-answer analysis still have their 5,000-row limits and the inventory remains unfiltered by programme selection; this does not complete the roadmap or full-filter parity. No production flag, cleanup, index or deployment is enabled.

## Resumable respondent lookup — 7 November 2026 (local)

The people-only name/email/mobile lookup now has a gated 24-hour snapshot path sharing the cohort index scanner, temporary membership, calendar/review ledger, authorization and cleanup. It preserves reviewed mapping, exact email/mobile and partial-name matching, accessible-event and form/programme scope, confirmed-person deduplication versus unresolved responses, and masked contact hints. Private membership holds only the displayed names, masked hints, references and sampled source/link/mapping/person versions; confirmed results precede unresolved results, whose snapshot ordering is by index key rather than the old in-memory form/response ordering. Each result page rechecks its samples and the ledger; aggregate counts and other contributors still depend on generation markers, with asynchronous trigger lag and out-of-band bypasses not atomically fenced. Jobs reject more than 50,000 scanned records, 600 distinct calendar/review paths, or an oversized per-person projection without partial results. The legacy 5,000-record refusal remains when either flag is off. Snapshot cancellation now checks current people authorization but no longer requires unchanged source/calendar generations, so invalidated jobs can be cancelled; repeated cancellation is idempotent while the job remains cancelled.

## Resumable cohort snapshots — 7 November 2026 (local)

The local people-only first-known and cohort heatmap now share the existing 24-hour snapshot job, feature/cleanup gates, resumable client and cleanup path. They scan indexed submissions in Sydney submission-date order in 100-record batches, retain private person membership, first-known dates and one source reference per matching year (no copied contact or answer payloads), then require a separate validation step before any results. Earliest known history covers accessible forms/events from the chosen history start; form/event/reviewed-category/booking-type/series filters narrow qualifying period submissions, not earlier history. Coverage, incomplete history and partial years are disclosed. Person cell pages require people permission; aggregate Excel/PDF also require export permission and a final same-snapshot authorization/version check. With flags off the existing explicit 5,000-record path remains.

A sharded private ledger records versions of accessible and unavailable calendar events, and, when programme filters apply, assignment and referenced category documents including missing ones. Changed dimensions detected during scanning, finalization or any later cell/aggregate/export read force a restart. Finalization and each result/export recheck all entries transactionally; scans check only current-batch documents plus global source/index/identity generations, not the entire accumulated ledger. The ledger currently refuses more than **600 distinct calendar/review document paths**, with no partial result; validation reads up to that many documents plus ledger shards, so large-event and production cost/capacity remain unverified. Missing source/index changes use existing generation markers; asynchronous source-trigger lag and out-of-band writes that bypass generation updates can still undermine a claim of atomic all-history freshness. Do not claim that limitation is solved. Cleanup explicitly deletes the new ledger descendants as well as member documents before their parent, never source, people, links or audit records. The scheduler/flags remain **off and undeployed**; flags alone do not prove cleanup is running.

Both cohort and multi-event snapshot scans additionally refuse scopes exceeding **50,000 indexed records**, with no partial report; controls disclose this limit. Synthetic tests set job progress at the boundary and verify both paths reject the next record without exposing results. Cohort tests also match the existing report across a Sydney daylight-saving boundary and verify concurrent duplicate steps have only one successful commit. The first concurrency test attempt ran before the demo emulator finished starting and failed to connect; it passed once the emulator reported readiness. This is a conservative safety ceiling, not a measured production capacity.

## Temporary analysis snapshot decision — 7 November 2026

The user approved private derived analysis snapshots lasting 24 hours, with local design for cleanup of temporary snapshot data only. Original responses, identity records and audit records must never be deleted by that cleanup. Production cleanup activation remains separately approval-gated; this does not resolve source-data retention clocks or backup/deletion policy.

The local multi-event prototype uses 100-index-row resumable steps, private per-person membership records, expiry checks, current permissions and source/index/identity/calendar/review fences. Incomplete jobs are not returned as completed reports. The local `insightsMultiSnapshotCleanup` scheduled function calls a bounded, retryable helper only if `MWA_INSIGHTS_MULTI_CLEANUP_ENABLED=true`; it logs selected jobs, deleted members and pending work and propagates errors. The query selects expired temporary jobs and the helper deletes up to 100 members per invocation per job, then the parent after members are gone. It is not a callable endpoint. An active step checks expiry and state transactionally, and cleanup marks the job before deleting members; this fences concurrent work without a separate lease. The snapshot route requires both `MWA_INSIGHTS_MULTI_SNAPSHOT_ENABLED=true` and `MWA_INSIGHTS_MULTI_CLEANUP_ENABLED=true`; both default off. Off mode preserves the explicit 250-per-event comparison. Never enable either without separately approved, deployed and monitored cleanup: flags alone cannot prove the scheduler is installed or healthy. Logical 24-hour expiry does not physically remove data, and Firestore parent TTL does not delete subcollections. No TTL, scheduler, index, rules or deployment was activated. The separate historical analytics plan records release prerequisites.

## Local implementation checkpoint — form posters — 7 November 2026

The user chose Cloudflare and confirmed whole-image Small/Medium/Large sizing, Medium by default, below the title and above the introduction. Local Form Builder changes support optional PNG/JPEG/WebP posters, replacement/removal, an optional screen-reader description and bounded responsive sizing with `object-fit: contain`. Previews, published forms and direct-share forms place the image above the introduction; legacy forms without posters remain unchanged. The server validates raster format, decodes and strips metadata by re-encoding as WebP, limits input and output to 2 MiB, each dimension to 4,096 pixels and total pixels to 16 million, and rejects animated images. Form Builder authorization and revision checks apply; uploads are stored in private R2 objects. Public poster delivery rechecks that the associated form remains published, unexpired and points to the same object; admin draft previews require Form Builder access and recheck it after retrieval. Both deliveries use no-store responses. Once sent, an image cannot be recalled from a visitor's device.

Each upload first creates a private `_registrationPosterCleanup` intent with its unique object key, before any R2 write. A successful form transaction atomically consumes that intent as it installs the poster reference. Interrupted or failed uploads retain `upload_pending` intents instead of risking deletion after an ambiguous commit. Saving a replacement or removal atomically records the old object's `cleanup_pending` task before attempting deletion; failure is explicitly reported and remains queued. Firestore rules deny direct client access to these tasks. Synthetic mock-R2/emulator tests verified intent existence before the write, interrupted writes, conflicts and retained recovery records. A simulated manual recovery assumed quiesced writers; it does not provide production fencing. Recovery remains disabled, as stated in the newer checkpoint above.

Runtime non-secret names are `MWA_R2_ACCOUNT_ID` and `MWA_R2_POSTER_BUCKET`; Secret Manager bindings are `MWA_R2_ACCESS_KEY_ID` and `MWA_R2_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY`. Credentials require bucket-scoped object read/write/delete access. No values belong in this document, source or chat. The two secrets are bound to the shared `websiteCmsApi`, so absent secret resources/runtime access can block deploying that function even when posters are unused. Missing runtime configuration produces an explicit error when posters are used. Real R2 configuration and delivery require separate validation and separately authorized deployment; this local work does not establish a successful release.

## Local implementation checkpoint — respondent lookup and first-known reporting — 7 November 2026

The approved Phase-2 screen-first slice adds on-screen lookup by reviewed mapped respondent name, email or mobile, plus first-known/returning respondent reporting. Names are case/whitespace-normalized partial matches; email and mobile use conservative exact normalization. Results distinguish current explicitly confirmed respondents from unresolved matching response candidates, mask contact hints and never automatically confirm links or merges. Results can select a confirmed respondent for the existing timeline. Missing/conflicting mappings, stale source/index data, unavailable or inaccessible events and unindexed history are disclosed, not treated as identified people. Queries or contacts are not written into audit records; no person-detail download or new permission was added.

The period uses Sydney submission-date boundaries, and a separately selected history start defines the accessible indexed window. First-known means the earliest current confirmed indexed submission **within that window**, never first-ever. Returning means a current confirmed indexed submission before the period in that same window, across any form or accessible event, not necessarily the same event/programme. People are deduplicated across responses and forms; reviewed merge/undo affects subsequent results. Eventless forms are included; events missing from the current calendar or inaccessible to the viewer are excluded with coverage disclosure. Private calendar details remain restricted to Events & Bookings; aggregate, scoped detail and volunteer permissions do not confer people access. New reports remain protected by the existing people permission and live permission/calendar rechecks. UI clearing on observed revocation/session change is not an instantaneous push or proof that an already sent, still-valid token is cancelled.

**Bounds and limitations:** each search or report checks at most 5,000 indexed records within a selected history window of up to ten years, matching the existing aggregate report capacity; more is explicitly rejected before a partial result. Lists show 25 records per page. Each page recomputes and rechecks the full bounded scope, so production read cost and latency at the ceiling are not verified. Noncanonical submission timestamp strings are excluded as stale where encountered; legacy or unindexed dates may also be missed by the indexed date query. Stale/unmapped/unlinked or inaccessible records and history before the chosen start can change both counts and classifications. The existing asynchronous source-marker lag caveat remains; no atomic historical snapshot is claimed.

## Local implementation checkpoint — people-authorized respondent review and merge — 7 November 2026

The user clarified: “system should match but merge should be done by superadmin or admin after review”, and selected all admins with existing `form-insights-people` access as reviewers. This supersedes older Super-Admin-only identity-review restrictions; aggregate, scoped detail view/export and volunteer access alone confer no identity-review authority. Candidates remain suggestions, never automatic merges. This authorizes local implementation only; no real user grants or production identity operations were authorized.

`Website/functions/form-insights.js` independently authorizes respondent endpoints with `form-insights-people` rather than requiring aggregate access, and provides reviewer-owned bounded revision inspection without allowing reviewers to edit mappings. `Website/functions/insights-respondent.js` transactionally rechecks the UID-bound active allowlist, expiry, people permission, user profile and login policy for identity mutations. `Website/website/InsightsPeople.jsx`, `InsightsRespondents.jsx`, `firebase-bridge.jsx`, `AdminConsole.jsx` and `FormInsights.jsx` provide a separately accessible review entry and revision inspection; existing mapping writes remain Super Admin-only.

Reviewed whole-record merge and conflict-aware undo are now implemented locally, superseding older statements below that describe them as unimplemented. Preview displays affected linked-response counts and source references, matching contact types and warnings. It requires both respondents to have current explicitly linked responses and a shared exact reviewed email or mobile; a shared contact is not proof of identity. Confirmation revalidates the preview, current permission, person/link/source/mapping versions and evidence transactionally. It atomically moves derived links and contact-bucket pointers, marks the source person merged, updates identity generation and writes a private versioned ledger and metadata-only audit. Source answers and consent are not changed. Undo checks the operation, person revisions, link set and integrity, and source/mapping evidence; later changes cause a conflict rather than being overwritten. History exposes the target's latest operation reference for recovery. No automatic linking or merging is enabled.

**Limit:** at most 40 combined link records, including inactive/tombstone records, are supported per merge; oversized merges are rejected before writes, not partially applied. Stale or unresolved linked evidence must be reinspected/reconfirmed first. This is not an unlimited or resumable merge. The asynchronous source-generation marker may lag an out-of-band write, so this is not a guarantee of an atomic historical snapshot. Source corrections, mapping changes, subsequent reviews or merges may prevent undo. The UI clears private state on observed session loss, but signing out alone does not prove cancellation of an already sent request with a still-valid token; server authorization is rechecked.

Affected merge files: `Website/functions/insights-respondent.js`, `insights-respondent.test.js`, `form-insights.js`, `Website/website/InsightsRespondents.jsx`, `Centralised Calendar/firestore.rules`, `Centralised Calendar/functions/rules.emulator.test.js` and this handover. Backend syntax and `git diff --check` passed, with existing line-ending warnings.

## Local implementation checkpoint — scoped Insights response-detail export — 7 November 2026

The user resolved the scope question: when an assignment selects both forms and events, a response must match **both** an assigned form and its own saved event ID. Form-only and event-only assignments are also supported. This authorizes the local implementation, not actual grants or production operations.

The separate admin-console entry `Scoped response-detail export` has its own server authorization, independent of aggregate Insights, people-search, aggregate export and volunteer grants. Super Admin can assign a currently active, UID-bound existing admin a nonempty selection of up to 30 form IDs and/or 30 event IDs, an expiry at the end of a selected Sydney day, and can revoke or revise the assignment. Super Admin also explicitly selects a scope for their own export. Form/event intersection uses each saved response's event ID, never the current form link. Assigned admins cannot manage grants or widen scope. No real assignments were made. Event IDs in response rows are omitted from the resulting workbook to avoid leaking private calendar identifiers; an explicit event assignment itself is visible to its assignee.

The Excel workflow scans source responses in batches of 100; it exports the entire assigned scope rather than an individual page, with no free-text or date filter. It explicitly refuses more than 20,000 scanned responses or malformed/missing saved snapshots rather than producing a partial file. Live bound-admin access, assignment revision, expiry, revocation, scope and source-generation markers are checked during preparation and immediately before download. In-memory answer data is cleared on cancellation, failure or access loss. Metadata-only audit events contain no answers, contacts or filter text. Questions, consent terms, current answers, submitted form title/purpose/revision and correction timestamps/revision are retained; digest, private original-answer backups and editor identity are excluded. Exported answers do not change consent or imply identity merging. Jobs expire after 24 hours; their stored records contain progress/scope metadata only, and no automatic metadata cleanup was enabled.

## Latest local implementation checkpoint — current calendar event dates — 7 November 2026

The user explicitly chose **the current calendar date for all indexed responses linked to an event**, rather than a submission-time historical event date; submission-date reports stay available. Old historical event dates cannot reliably be reconstructed. This supersedes older checkpoints describing event-date reports as grouped by the date stored during indexing. Locally, an event-date report now selects current Sydney calendar dates, then joins indexed responses by event ID, regardless of each index row's older `eventAt`. The same current date is used for month/year totals, filters, report rows, exports and the event inventory. Responses whose event is missing, inaccessible, eventless or lacks a usable current date are excluded from event-date reports, without inventing a date or exclusion count. Submission-date filtering remains unchanged. Current calendar titles/status are shown only within the existing calendar permissions; Insights access alone does not grant private event details.

The read-time join uses at most 5,000 calendar events, queries event IDs in groups of up to 30 and explicitly refuses more than 5,000 indexed responses instead of returning a partial report. Calendar document-version fingerprints bind pagination to the relevant calendar scope, and are rechecked during report preparation; an event-date report and its separate inventory must also have matching calendar fingerprints. Relevant calendar changes between pages cause a 409 and require rerunning the report. This does not claim a historical time-travel snapshot: generation checks do not make out-of-band source writes instantaneous, and future changes after report generation remain possible. Large event ranges can require many bounded sequential queries; production scale and actual provider indexes have not been tested. Source/index reconciliation still does **not** check calendar dates or prove complete history, and its status explicitly states that limitation.

The Insights UI, API definitions and the shared filter text passed to both PDF and Excel exports now explain the current-date basis, unavailable-date exclusions, indexed-data lag, current calendar attributes and incomplete coverage. The Excel summary was inspected by synthetic round-trip; the PDF path uses the same filter but a rendered PDF was not separately inspected. No production reindex/backfill is needed solely for this read-time date change, and none was performed. No permissions, rules, ingestion or deployment settings were changed.

## Latest local implementation checkpoint — resumable historical revision scans — 7 November 2026

The previous mapping checkpoint's 1,000-response refusal has been replaced locally with Super Admin-only discovery and revision inspection jobs. Each user-driven step scans at most 100 response snapshots and persists its cursor and progress; job status, continuation and cancellation can be recovered after reloading the same browser tab. Discovery stores per-revision counts in separate job documents and returns completed results in pages of 100 revisions. Running, cancelled or invalidated scans do not expose partial discoveries or authorize mapping review. A source-generation marker and the current form's update timestamp bind each job to its scope; concurrent source/form changes invalidate it, while atomic checkpoints reject stale concurrent steps. The UI displays progress, incomplete/error state, continuation and cancellation.

Saving, reviewing and copying require a completed, currently valid inspection of the relevant revision. Copying additionally requires a completed inspection of the earlier revision and remains an explicit draft containing only exact-definition matches; both inspections must share the same source generation and form version. Mapping writes transactionally recheck generation, form update time and mapping versions. Existing consent, schema-conflict, audit and coverage-invalidation safeguards remain; inspection jobs contain field definitions and counts, not answer values. The previous limitations remain: forms retain only their current schema and old revisions without surviving response snapshots may not be discoverable; absent/malformed or conflicting snapshots cannot be reviewed. Direct out-of-band Firestore writes depend on asynchronous triggers for generation changes, so an unchanged generation is not an absolute guarantee against a write before its trigger runs. Job metadata persists; no automated cleanup, ingestion, repair or deletion was enabled.

Affected in this increment: `Website/functions/insights-mapping.js`, `Website/functions/insights-mapping.test.js`, `Website/functions/form-insights.js`, `Website/website/InsightsMapping.jsx`, `Website/website/registrations-render.test.mjs`, and this handover. ## Latest local implementation checkpoint — Phase-1 historical question mapping — 7 November 2026

The user explicitly chose **a separate reviewed mapping for every revision**, with an explicit copy of an earlier mapping as a reviewable draft, never automatic inheritance or approval. A Super Admin-only mapping screen and API now discover and inspect revisions of a specified form, display original field IDs, types, labels, descriptions, options and consent terms, and save semantic-role/question-concept drafts. Supported conservative roles distinguish respondent name/email/mobile, participant name and household size; consent fields cannot be mapped in this foundation. Concept keys are explicit and optional. A saved draft must be separately marked reviewed; saving or copying a previously reviewed mapping returns that revision to draft. Copying is allowed only from an earlier available mapping and transfers mappings only for fields whose full displayed definitions match exactly. It does not activate analysis, person matching or merging.

Actual source limitation: forms store the current revision; historical field snapshots exist only on responses that captured them. Revision discovery checks at most 1,000 response documents for a form and reports unknown/missing revision IDs; revision inspection checks at most 1,000 response snapshots for that revision. Exceeding either bound returns an explicit 422 rather than a partial result. Earlier revisions with no surviving response snapshots may not be discoverable. Missing/malformed snapshots are **unavailable**; inconsistent snapshots are **conflict** and cannot be reviewed. No questions or options are invented. The UI also accepts a known revision number when discovery exceeds its bound.

Mapping records are private and keyed by form and revision, with optimistic version checks, snapshot hashes and an explicit reviewed/draft status. Changes are audited without answers or contact data. Changes invalidate the existing derived index-generation marker and coverage version, so stale report cursors and reconciliation freshness are not silently presented as current; original forms, responses, consent, answers and index rows are not changed. The mapping endpoint and controls use the existing Super Admin boundary; no permissions/rules were broadened. As with reconciliation, arbitrary direct Firestore writers depend on asynchronous source-change triggers; snapshot integrity is not claimed where those triggers have not yet run.

Affected files: `Website/functions/insights-mapping.js` (new), `Website/functions/insights-mapping.test.js` (new), `Website/functions/form-insights.js`, `Website/website/InsightsMapping.jsx` (new), `Website/website/FormInsights.jsx`, and `Website/website/registrations-render.test.mjs`. The approved plan remains `Form Builder - Historical Analytics Plan and Matrix - 2026-11-06.md`. The previous checkpoint's mapping-UI-foundation gap is partially addressed by this local revision-review foundation, not by activated mapped reporting.

Verification: the managed local Firestore demo emulator run of `insights-mapping.test.js`, `form-insights.test.js` and `registrations-api.test.js` passed **19/19, zero skips**. It covered explicit draft/review, no inheritance, exact-definition copying as draft, missing/conflicting revisions, optimistic conflicts, consent rejection, private-answer exclusion from audit/API output, coverage invalidation, no source/index mutation, revision discovery and denied non-owner access. `Website/website/registrations-render.test.mjs` passed **8/8, zero skips**, including owner/non-owner controls. `npm run build` in `Website` built both Website and embedded Calendar. Backend syntax and `git diff --check` passed; Git reported pre-existing LF-to-CRLF warnings. No production reads/writes, backfill, ingestion activation, reindex, deletion, permission grant, deployment, commit, push or real messages occurred.

## Latest local implementation checkpoint — Phase-1 resumable source/index reconciliation — 7 November 2026

The user directed that Insights remains the priority and YouTube stays deferred. This increment adds a bounded, resumable reconciliation scan between `registrationForms/{formId}/responses` and the private `_registrationInsights` index. It captures the current form-ID scope and a stable generation baseline; a moving baseline refuses start. Source and index scans advance in persisted batches of at most 50 records across at most 5,000 forms. A single atomic lock prevents overlapping jobs, and the Super Admin can recover the active job by refreshing status after a page reload.

The scan records separate counts and hashed, metadata-only issue entries for missing index rows, stale index rows, orphan rows whose source response is absent (including deleted-source cases), unindexable source responses, and malformed/mis-keyed index rows. It compares indexed source-derived fields (form/response IDs, reference, form-title snapshot, event ID, submission date, search text and index version); it does not store answers or contact details in job/issue metadata. Source and index documents are read-only to the scan. The scan writes only job/cursor/issue, coverage/freshness and audit metadata; it never indexes, repairs or deletes responses or index rows.

Generation tracking uses 64 source and 64 index shards plus a forms-catalog generation. Normal Form Builder submissions and answer corrections increment the source marker in their existing transaction; creation of a new form increments the catalog marker. Index changes increment the index marker in the same index transaction. Firestore response/form triggers also record external response writes and form create/delete events; those triggers are asynchronous for writes made outside the API, so this unreleased implementation does not claim a production-grade instantaneous signal for arbitrary direct Firestore writers. The scan's final transaction reads the job and all generation markers together. A changed vector produces `invalidated`/`changed-during-scan`; an unchanged vector produces `scan_complete`, after which status rechecks the markers and can report `changed-since-scan`. Even a stable scan remains **Coverage incomplete**: it cannot prove that unlinked legacy responses or identity/question mappings are complete. No count equality is presented as proof of complete history.

The endpoints and controls are Super Admin-only, enforced server-side with the existing owner check; render tests also verify ordinary Insights admins do not see the controls. Existing Insights/event permissions and private/unconfirmed calendar boundaries are unchanged. The source/index ingestion gate remains unchanged; no production indexing/backfill/deletion, access grant, rules change, deployment, commit or push occurred.

Affected files: `Website/functions/form-insights.js`, `Website/functions/insights-state.js`, `Website/functions/registrations-api.js`, `Website/functions/index.js`, `Website/functions/form-insights.test.js`, `Website/functions/registrations-api.test.js`, `Website/website/FormInsights.jsx`, and `Website/website/registrations-render.test.mjs`. The initially attempted metadata paths were corrected to valid Firestore collection/document paths before validation.

Verification: `firebase emulators:exec --only firestore --project demo-mwa-insights --config "Centralised Calendar/firebase.json" "node --test Website/functions/form-insights.test.js Website/functions/registrations-api.test.js"` passed **17/17, zero skips** on the local demo Firestore emulator. Synthetic cases covered all five issue categories, owner denial, active-job lock/status recovery, source/index/catalog mutation invalidation, stable post-scan freshness, no source/index repair, and resumability across the 50-record source and index batch boundaries. The same run verified transactional generation increments for new-form creation, submission and answer correction. `Website/website/registrations-render.test.mjs` passed **8/8, zero skips**, including owner/non-owner controls. `npm run build` in `Website` built both Website and embedded Calendar. Backend/test syntax checks and `git diff --check` passed; Git reported existing LF-to-CRLF normalization warnings in other dirty files. The emulator was stopped; ports 8080 and 9099 were confirmed closed.

Remaining Phase-1 work is not complete: define and build the approved historical field/question mapping UI foundation and remaining source dimensions/definitions, and resolve the separately authorized scope for response-detail exports before exposing answer/contact data. Mapping semantics must remain review-only; do not infer identity or merge people. Event title/date snapshot drift and arbitrary external-write trigger latency are not certified by this response/index-field reconciliation. The earlier checkpoints below that call reconciliation outstanding are superseded by this entry; YouTube remains deferred. Canonical `main` remains at `b68afcc`, with pre-existing broad dirty work preserved and all work local/uncommitted/unpushed/undeployed.

Do not assign volunteer access to an existing admin or use it to bypass module restrictions. If a volunteer later becomes an admin, explicitly retire the volunteer assignment and apply the intended admin grants; do not silently union roles or broaden access.

Recheck grants for asynchronous export downloads; already downloaded files cannot be recalled.

This is a terminology change, not a widening of authority or change to the protected identity. Internal identifiers/compatibility fields and factual Google Cloud/GitHub IAM role names may remain unchanged. Preserve historical ownership-transfer evidence in documentation.
