## Booking approval email — latest design

New public booking submissions email Events@muhammadiwelfare.org.au and copy admin@muhammadiwelfare.org.au. Approve/Reject links use `/booking-response`. The user explicitly selected **no sign-in, one confirmation click**.

- Random 256-bit bearer token; only its SHA-256 hash is stored in server-only `_bookingEmailActions` documents.
- Expires after 48 hours, scoped to one booking revision, consumed atomically with the decision.
- GET displays a confirmation page and never changes a booking. POST confirms the action. Do not replace this with approval on GET: email scanners follow links.
- Approval rechecks conflicts inside the transaction. Conflicting submission emails show disabled approval and active rejection. Stale, expired, removed, already-decided and consumed requests are rejected safely.
- Approved requests send the existing requester email plus ICS. Rejections send the existing decision email. The page reports SMTP send failure rather than claiming delivery. SMTP acceptance is not proof of inbox delivery.
- Audit records honestly identify an email-link action, not a signed-in individual. Anyone with the link can act; do not put tokens in docs or forward them outside the intended team.
- Previously delivered emails retain their old authenticated links. Only new submissions get the new links. Arbitrary incoming free-text emails do not create bookings.

Main files: `Centralised Calendar/functions/emailBookingLink.js`, `bookingEmailDecision.js` (older authenticated action), `notifications.js`, `index.js`; hosting rewrite in `Website/firebase.json`.

## Build, checks and release reference

Use Node 22 with matching native dependency architecture. This Windows ARM machine previously used x64 Node at:
`C:/Users/sajja/OneDrive/Archieved/Documents/ChatGPT/MWA Project/mwa-build-runtime/node-v22.23.4-win-x64`
Check availability before relying on that machine-specific path. Canonical source is outside the assistant scratch writable root; use the approved escalation workflow when required rather than editing another copy.

From `Website/`:
```powershell
npm run build
node website/admin-team-render.test.mjs
node --test '../Centralised Calendar/functions/emailBookingLink.test.js' '../Centralised Calendar/functions/bookingEmailDecision.test.js'
firebase deploy --only hosting:website,hosting:calendar --project project-7a4fb531-414f-4ade-88e --non-interactive
```
The build produces website `dist/` and embedded `dist/calendar-app/`. Legacy calendar hosting uses `calendar-redirect/`, not another full app. Do not pass a relative calendar outDir carelessly: it resolves against the calendar Vite root.

From `Centralised Calendar/`, choose only relevant backend targets, for example:
```powershell
node --test functions/*.test.js
firebase deploy --only functions:bookingEmailAction,functions:submitBooking --project project-7a4fb531-414f-4ade-88e --non-interactive
```
For website API changes, from `Website/`:
```powershell
node --test functions/*.test.js
firebase deploy --only functions:mwa-website:websiteCmsApi --project project-7a4fb531-414f-4ade-88e --non-interactive
```
Emulator-dependent tests require the Firestore emulator; skipped tests are not passed tests. For permission changes run the relevant rules/emulator checks, including `Website/website/module-access-rules.test.mjs`. Inspect current config before using older emulator commands. Deploy rules from the calendar folder only when changed. Avoid unrestricted Firebase deployment and do not upgrade dependencies incidentally.

After deployment, inspect completion output and live behaviour. A vanished terminal session does not prove success; verify remote function state/assets. Test without modifying real bookings, admin permissions or sending live messages unless the user specifically authorises that test. No secrets in logs or screenshots.

### Form Builder and public Registrations - 3 November 2026

**Status: deployed successfully after user authorisation at 12:57 Sydney on 3 November; source and this release record are included in the same local commit. No Git push was requested or performed.** Initial worktree was clean on `main` at `1f3e703`, matching the local `origin/main` reference. Existing live donation/booking functionality was not changed or exercised against production.

Confirmed scope and decisions:
- User requested reuse of the Community Connect Australia form builder for both event-related and standalone community inputs. Source was located in `SAJ0786/CommunityConnect-WebApp`, revision `97ee4514022f331bdc1af375dddb09b2f91b4603`. Adapted its question contract, templates, consent-last ordering and answer renderer; did not connect the apps' databases or copy its event capacity, attendee authentication or approval workflows.
- User explicitly required a separate admin module named **Form Builder**, not Website CMS, and a public **Registrations** panel alongside social media. Admin route: `/admin-login?module=form-builder`; public list/detail: `/registrations/` and `/registrations/?form=<id>`.
- Forms support ten question types, basic/meal/feedback starting templates, help text, required questions, choices, consent terms, question ordering and preview. Admins can save drafts, publish, unpublish and review private responses. Drafts are not listed publicly. No user-defined saved-template library, capacity/payment workflow, attendee account or response email integration was added.
- Every saved form requires an expiry date. It remains open through that Sydney calendar day and closes at the following midnight, respecting DST. API listing/detail/submission enforce expiry; the open homepage/list view also removes expired entries without a refresh.
- Calendar linking is optional and restricted to confirmed public events (including the existing always-public MWA Programs policy). Select an event date to find events. Private/pending booking details are not exposed by this new permission. Linking never creates, edits, approves or reserves a calendar booking; form receipt is not an event approval.
- New `form-builder` access is explicit for non-owners. Both module catalogs, Admin Console guards, Admin Team selections and server checks agree; the fixed legacy 13-module baseline remains unchanged. Added Form Builder to activity recording and audit-module filters.

Storage/API:
- `registrationForms/{id}` stores definitions; `responses/{responseId}` stores private question/terms snapshots, answers and submission references, preserving the original questions after later edits.
- `registrationListings/{id}` holds only published public list metadata. `_registrationRateLimits` stores hashed IP/time-bucket counters; no raw IP is persisted. Existing default-deny rules already prevented client access; explicit deny rules were added for all three collections and response descendants. Reads/writes go through the API.
- `/api/registrations/**` is handled inside the existing website API. Definition saves have revision-conflict protection and transactional publication/auditing. Public responses are server-validated, idempotent by submission key, limited to ten new submissions per IP per 15-minute bucket, and include a honeypot. Listing/forms/responses use 50-record pagination with Load more; no composite index was needed in emulator checks.
- Only supported public fields leave the public endpoints. Admin endpoints verify the existing admin role, live allowlist, expiry, password-session policy and `form-builder` permission. Authenticated CMS-only and legacy admins cannot read responses or manage forms.
- Rate-limit records have an expiry timestamp, but no new Firestore TTL policy or data-retention automation was configured. Response retention follows the existing manual administrative model; no deletion/export workflow was requested.

Affected files:
- New website UI: `Website/website/FormBuilder.jsx`, `Registrations.jsx`, `RegistrationFields.jsx`, `registration-client.js`, `registrations.css`; integration in `AdminConsole.jsx`, `firebase-bridge.jsx`, `index.html`.
- New API/shared model: `Website/functions/registrations-api.js`, `registration-model.mjs`; route added in `Website/functions/index.js`.

### Form response management, Excel export and direct sharing - 3 November 2026

**Status at implementation checkpoint: implemented and verified locally; final production rebuild completed, then awaiting release authorisation. Superseded by the successful follow-up release checkpoint below.** Preserve the earlier release at `9063c44` and the thank-you wording feedback above.

Confirmed behaviour and decisions:
- Responses remain ordered by original submission time descending, including pagination, filtering, Excel and after corrections. Editing does not turn an older response into a newer submission.
- Admins can choose a form question (including Name) or All answers, enter case-insensitive "Answer contains" text and Apply filter. Filtering fetches every response page, not just the loaded page. Results are displayed 50 at a time; the matching count includes all matches. Clear filter restores the ordinary paginated response view. Reapply a filter to include later submissions.
- Excel exports the entire active filtered snapshot, including matches not displayed. With no filter it fetches all response pages using one submission-time cutoff. Empty exports contain headers and current question documentation. No partial subset is silently downloaded: filtering/export fails explicitly above 20,000 responses, on page loops or on fetch/permission failure. The ordinary unfiltered list remains paginated without that export cap.
- Workbook sheets: Export summary (cutoff, scope, count and filter), Responses (references, Sydney dates/times, saved form metadata, correction metadata and answers), Questions (saved labels, options, help, required settings, consent terms and revisions). Different historical question definitions receive separate answer columns; text is stored literally, phone leading zeroes are preserved, numbers/dates use appropriate Excel cell types. Uses existing dynamically imported ExcelJS; no application dependency change.
- User explicitly chose **correct other fields; keep submitted consent unchanged**. Admin editing uses the questions/options saved with the response, not today's form definition. The server validates the full answers, rejects consent changes/omissions, checks edit revision conflicts, preserves reference/submission time/form revision/question/consent snapshots and original submission digest, and backs up the first pre-correction answers privately. The API does not expose that backup or digest. Original anonymous retries remain idempotent and do not undo corrections.
- Corrections store edit revision/time/admin UID and transactionally audit the changed question IDs, never answer values. Exports perform a final protected preparation/audit request to recheck live Form Builder access before initiating the browser download. Audits record preparation and a client-reported count/filter flag, not claimed OS download completion; search text is not logged. No new permission or direct-client Firestore access was introduced.
- Saved, published, unexpired forms offer **Copy link**, **WhatsApp** and **Text message** in the form list and saved editor. Sharing always references the saved form, not unsaved editor changes. Draft/expired forms show guidance instead. Copy failures are visible and the selectable URL remains available; WhatsApp/SMS open the user's messaging app with the link prefilled, without automatically sending a message. SMS links account for iOS/Android body separators.
- Shared URL: `/registrations/?form=<id>&shared=1`. It opens the specified anonymous form directly, hides the website menu/notice/footer and registration-list navigation, and requires no account. After submission the form-only view contains just the Thank you heading and "We have received your response. Your reference number is [actual reference]." Existing ordinary registration links, homepage panel, closure/expiry checks and private admin boundaries remain intact.
- Session/module changes clear private response/filter/editor state and invalidate outstanding response/export operations. Export progress/cancellation is visible. No real response, permission grant, email, WhatsApp/SMS message, booking or payment was created or changed.

Affected files:
- `Website/website/FormBuilder.jsx`, `Registrations.jsx`, `RegistrationShare.jsx`, `registration-share.mjs`, `registration-response-model.mjs`, `registration-exports.mjs`, `registrations.css`, `index.html`.
- `Website/functions/registrations-api.js`: optional response cutoff, protected `/admin/<form>/export` preparation and `PUT /admin/<form>/responses/<response>` corrections. Existing per-request Form Builder authentication and same-origin mutation checks apply.
- Tests: `Website/functions/registrations-api.test.js`, `Website/website/registration-exports.test.mjs`, `registration-share.test.mjs`, `registrations-render.test.mjs`; this original shared handover.

### Historical email verification delivery failure diagnosis - 6 November 2026 (superseded by user confirmation on 7 November)

At the time, the user reported "We could not send the verification code. Try again later or choose mobile verification." Read-only, recipient/content-filtered Cloud Run logs for `startBookingVerification` showed Gmail SMTP authentication rejection `535 5.7.8 Username and Password not accepted`. This records the earlier failure before code confirmation, not an incorrect OTP or App Check failure. The Function was ACTIVE and bound SMTP_USER version 2 and SMTP_PASS version 3; metadata confirmed these were the latest enabled versions. The OTP HMAC secret was bound to version 1. No secret values, email contents or codes were read; no test message, production write, configuration change or deployment was performed during that diagnostic. On 7 November, the user confirmed actual email and SMS verification are now working; that user report supersedes the prior current-status conclusion but does not independently verify the full booking lifecycle, notifications or App Check enforcement.

Next: inspect shared SMTP consumers before changing any credentials, as clarified in the Codex task list below. Do not add a new shared SMTP_PASS version or revoke the old app password without checking services that read latest and obtaining approval for the chosen scope. A successful real-message check needs separate approval; Gmail's credential rejection is confirmed but inbox delivery and the fix are not verified. This diagnostic handover update is local pending publication.

### User-directed stop and remaining Codex tasks - 6 November 2026

At the time on 6 November, the user requested leaving SMTP unchanged and recording all remaining work for Codex.
